#68110 3 2005-05-20T11:58:05Z shoftee
Ok.... the quit thing is:

fscommand ("quit", "true");

and... the right-click menu (or also called a context menu) is a bit more difficult.
The problem is that you can't "disable" it completely.
The only thing you can do is shorten it a bit 

fscommand("showmenu", "false");

That removes all context menu items except "About Flash Player..." and "Settings...". Now... if you want to put your own things in the context menu... that's a lot more difficult (well not A LOT, but I'm too lazy to explain )

I hope I've helped

TS

#69003 2 2005-05-23T21:11:54Z shoftee
Well... technically, it should be possible to use the SAT. You just have to calculate the normal vectors of the square and triangle. How? Well if you have a triangle for example, the normal of each of the triangle's edges would be either the right-hand or left-hand normal of that edge, depending on the orientation of the triangle. If the orientation is CW, then you should use left-hand normals, and if it's CCW - right-hand normals. If you have problems determining the orientation, just ask.

Hope I've helped.

TS
#69161 4 2005-05-24T18:03:20Z shoftee
Hi,

After I used a pencil and a piece of paper for 10 minutes, I discovered that you actually don't need the orientation . That means that you just pick one of the two(left or right hand) normals and calculate the projection length of the edges onto it. There might be a way to skip some of the edges (maybe using voronoi regions), but this will have to do for now...

And don't worry, it's not so complicated.

#71316 2 2005-05-31T20:58:51Z shoftee
OK... basicly collision multisampling is used when small objects move too fast. So first of all you need to know how fast your objects could ever move . But you (probably) already know that .
What you need to do is:

1. For each step, check if the current particle moves faster than a certain limit.
2. If it does, you divide the big step into smaller ones and calculate where the object would be after each smaller step.
3. check if there is a collision for the new position of the object.

Without this, if the object moves too fast, it might miss a collision, or in other words, it might just step "over" another object and carry on.
The problem with multisampling is, that you have to limit the number of smaller steps. So technically if an object moves fast enough no time-unexpensive algorithm can "save" it. But if you make some adjustments (like max-speed limits), you wouldn't have such a problem.

I can't give you any AS cuz I haven't really tried to write such a thing. I have thought of multisampling before, but never used it. You might want to ask Raigan about code if you're too lazy . He should have some code (duh, he's used it in n )

Hope I've helped

TS

#71532 4 2005-06-01T20:13:08Z shoftee
No, that's not the way.
If you do it like that, after it collides it be twice as slower.

OK, you have the variables:

numdiv -> the number of smaller steps
i -> obvios
v -> the object's position (Vector2)
v2 -> the object's possible position (Vector2)
step -> the velocity vector (Vector2)
sstep -> the smaller velocity vector (Vector2)
( please read through the code and the explanation after it it's not complete, and if you just put it in there without looking at it you'll learn nothing )

var numdiv = 4;
var i;
var v = new Vector2(20, 20); // for example
var v2 = new Vector2();
var maxspeed = 10;
var step = new Vector2 (10, 10); // for example...
var sstep = new Vector2(0, 0);
if (step.len () > maxspeed) {
 sstep.x = step.x / numdiv;
 sstep.y = step.y / numdiv;
 v2 = v;
 for (i = 0; i < numdiv; i ++) {
  v2.x += sstep.x;
  v2.y += sstep.y;
  // here you do collision-check for object at position v2,
  // that means you check as if the object was at position v2
  // I'm doing a trace on where the object will be
  trace (v2.x + ", " + v2.y);
 }
}
Now, let me explain what exactly I am doing here. First I declare all the variables (duh). I'm using the Metanet Vector2 object .
After that, you check if the speed is too big. 10 * 10 + 10 * 10 = 200. Square root of 200 is approx. 14.14 (14.142135623731 to be exact ) > 10 so it enters the if.
Then you calculate the multisampling step. Since the big step is (10, 10) and numdiv is 4, so the smaller step is (2.5, 2.5).
Then it enters the for loop. For every i from 0 to 3 inclusive, it calculates the position and checks for collision.
When/If you find a collision be sure to break out of the main for loop .

I'm giving you a file. It's for Flash MX 2004. If you have an older one, tell me so I can convert it.

Now, there's no way I couldn't have helped, so:

Glad to have helped

TS

#73113 2 2005-06-07T23:13:13Z shoftee
Facing vector...
Well, in the slides, it says that the facing vector defines the "x" axis of the box

Let's examine what you're doing here...
boxA.px = boxA._x;
boxA.py = boxA._y;
boxA.xw = boxA._width / 2;
boxA.yw = boxA._height / 2;
// ...
boxA.dx = boxA.xw - boxA.px;
boxA.dy = boxA.py - boxA.py;

Now tell me, why the heck would you want to subtract the box's position from the box's halfwidth???

The vector you're looking for is a unit vector, that means its length is 1.
For an AABB(Axis Aligned Bounding Box) the facing vector is (1, 0).
And I'm gonna compare your code, with the code from the slides:

Your:
deltax = boxA.xw - boxB.px;
deltay = boxA.py - boxB.py;

Slides:
deltax = boxA.px - boxB.px;
deltay = boxA.py - boxB.py;

If you want code, take a look at the attached file.

Hope I've helped.

TS

#73278 6 2005-06-08T18:20:01Z shoftee
Now, firstly, you should use Math.pow() only if necessary. Instead you could just do:
Math.sqrt(ax * ax + ay * ay);
Even more, you don't even need to calculate a square root. Since you know that the vector (axis_x, axis_y) has to have a length 1, then your square root is already calculated for you:

axis_x = Math.SQRT2 / 2;
axis_y = Math.SQRT2 / 2;

since a 45 degree line with a length 1 has x and y of 0.7071.... and the square root of 2 is 1.414.... then you just have to divide by two and there you have it: no function calls! And no function calls means more speed.
Nevermind, with that little optimisation, we can carry on...

Ok, now you need to check for the third axis only when deltax < 0 and deltay > 0 (The hypotenuse is facing the up-right corner).
// triangle test code
If (deltax > 0 || deltay < 0) {
// already collides
} else {
// check for hypotenuse collision
}
boxA.xw = boxA._width / 2;
boxA.yw = boxA._height / 2;
boxA.dx = 1;
boxA.dy = 0;
boxB.xw = boxB._width / 2;
boxB.yw = boxB._height / 2;
boxB.dx = 1;
boxB.dy = 0;
function ProjectBox (box, ax, ay) {
	ix = box.xw * box.dx;
	iy = box.xw * box.dy;
	jx = box.yw * -box.dy;
	jy = box.yw * box.dx;
	dpi = ix * ax + iy * ay;
	dpj = jx * ax + jy * ay;
	return Math.abs (dpi) + Math.abs (dpj);
}
this.onEnterFrame = function () {
	txtMessage.text = '';
	boxA.px = boxA._x;
	boxA.py = boxA._y;
	boxB.px = boxB._x;
	boxB.py = boxB._y;
	deltax = boxA.px - boxB.px;
	deltay = boxA.py - boxB.py;
	axis_x = boxB.dx;
	axis_y = boxB.dy;
	asize = boxA.xw;
	bsize = ProjectBox (boxB, axis_x, axis_y);
	dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
	penAx = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
	if (penAx > 0) {
  // boxes overlap along axis;  check next axis
  txtMessage.text = 'Horizontal check passed...checking Vertical';
  axis_x = boxB.dy;
  axis_y = -boxB.dx;
  asize = boxA.yw;
  bsize = ProjectBox (boxB, axis_x, axis_y);
  dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
  penBx = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
  if (penBx > 0) {
  	if (deltax < 0 || deltay > 0) {
    txtMessage.text += '\nVertical check passed...collision detected';
  	} else {
    txtMessage.text += '\nVertical check passed...checking triangle';
    ax = boxB.xw;
    ay = boxB.yw;
    axis_x = Math.SQRT2 / 2;
    axis_y = -Math.SQRT2 / 2;
    asize = ProjectBox (boxA, axis_x, axis_y);
    bsize = ProjectBox (boxB, axis_x, axis_y);
    dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
    penTx = asize - dsize;
    if (penTx > 0) {
    	txtMessage.text += '\nTriangle check passed...collision detected.\n' + penTx;
    }
  	}
  }
	}
};

I've attached the fixed file if you need it.

And, one more thing. I'm not into that vector stuff so much, but I realize it's powerful. Also I'm actually happy there's people who have problems like this cuz I can help 'em. Otherwise this whole "Programming Tutorials" Forum would go to hell

TS

#79184 7 2005-06-28T10:50:29Z shoftee
So...
As I said before, using just one or two sin calls isn't that much of a big deal. Also, if you want to use vectors, don't forget that you will have to write a Vector object for that. And that'll be quite useless, assuming you'll use it for just one call. Also, implementing the vector stuff will be quite time-consuming(relatively, at least).
And, finally, use the built in Math.PI. It's more accurate and it doesn't make the movie slower. If you really don't want to use Math.PI(mainly because of the damn, annoyng "Math." part ), then use some variable instead of it:

pi = Math.PI;

That's all folks...

#82773 9 2005-07-11T20:21:34Z shoftee
Sorry I'm late, but I'm not so active on these forums nowadays. This will be the longest post I've written, so get ready.
For the source:
Oh, boy. We have some fixing to do.
Judging by the Flash Player publishing version you're using Flash MX 2004. Therefore, you can use some little optimizations that came with Flash MX:
  • USE INTERVALS! Dammit, they're much more easier to work with! I haven't used (or seen anyone use for that matter) onClipEvent for about 2 years or so! (since Flash MX was released)
  • Look at the attached file, that's how particle physics is supposed to be done (well, at least with vertlet integration )
  • Forget about per-movieclip actions!
  • Working code = first; Fancy graphics = later!
Now, let's start from the ground up.
First, you have Particles, each particle has the following properties:
  • .x - which is obvious;
  • .y - obvious;
  • .oldx - the previous x position of the object;
  • .oldy - the previous y position of the object;
  • .vx - obvious;
  • .vy - obvious;
  • .mass - this is almost too obvious;
    (next is the not so obvious properties)
  • .sList[] - in this array property you store references ("shortcuts") to the particles that the current particle is connected to;
  • .rList[] - this array holds the rest-lengths
  • .sid - this is the interval ID. I can use this to "stop" the particle whenever I want to (look up clearInterval() in the help panel).
So we have the basic relaxation function, and another relaxation function, that's not so basic. It's hard to explain what exactly the second one does differently from the first, but let's just say that it makes the movie work most of the times (instead some of the times).

Next, we have the most important function in the whole source file: the tick() function. This function is executed every 10 milliseconds or so, and it basicly does what your onClipEvent(enterFrame) is supposed to do, but much more times per second. In the function you do the collision with the stage(friction and bounce) and the relaxation, of course.

And last, but not least, we have the addStick() function. It connects the particles together and it sets the rest length between each connected pair.

SO, that's about the particle object. Now, you make a movieclip that the will actually "represent" the particles on the stage (i.e a black dot). Then yo give it a linkage name (i.e "point"). And finally, yuo do this:

Object.registerClass("point", Particle);

Here, Particle is what the Particle object is called.

After you call that line, every object that you "attach" (look up attachMovie() in the flash help panel) will be a particle. You connect the particles with the addStick() function and TADA you have a ragdoll.

Expected reactions:
  • WTF?!?!
  • *brain explodes*
  • GET A LIFE!
  • Q: What's with all the formatting (A: I just wanted everything to look... more formatted )
If you understood half of what I've said, then you have a chance of getting a life.
If you didn't understand anything, then you shouldn't play N or get deeper into this computer stuff, first, because you don't understand any of it, and second, because you just have a life (look at sig for more details).
If you understood most of the stuff, then you still have a chance in getting a life.
If you understood all of it, then welcome to the club

And that's definitely the longest post I've made. And don't worry, that'll be my longest post for a while I think .

P.S.: In the file I keep the arm and leg ends apart from each other. If you don't like it, just comment-out these two lines:
p2.addStick (p3, 200);
and
p5.addStick (p6, 200);

I'm also sending you another thing to show you what you can do with Particles.

TS

About onClipEvent(enterFrame):
People write tutorials using onClipEvent because it's much easier for beginners to understand it. Intervals are much more powerful because you can control the framerate with code. And I often have scope problems with intervals (search scope in the help panel)

Don't mention it . I like to help people who have problems with Flash (DUH! That's the only thing I'm good at )

#84048 20 2005-07-16T20:06:08Z shoftee
ms/f is milliseconds per frame.
It is written in the lower left corner of the N window.
The less ms/f you have the faster N runs, and the more ms/f you have, the slower N runs. Got it?
On my computer most levels run 30-40 ms/f . On most new computers N runs at about 8 ms/f.

#86088 2 2005-07-25T22:12:28Z shoftee
OK, I see your problem.
Let's examine a piece of your code, shall we?

boxA.px = boxA._x;
boxA.py = boxA._y;
boxA.xw = boxA._width / 2;
boxA.yw = boxA._height / 2;

lenA = Math.sqrt( boxA.xw * boxA.xw + boxA.yw * boxA.yw ); // longueur
boxA.dx = boxA.xw / lenA; // v. unit x
boxA.dy = boxA.yw / lenA; // v. unit y

Tell me, why are you using your box half-width and half-height to determine your box rotation?
Let's leave boxA's rotation to 0 and mess around with boxB's rotation.
If you want to make a 45 degree rotation, what do you need?
Your dx and dy will have to be equal to 1, and you will have to normalize the (dx, dy) vector. The vector length is Math.sqrt(1*1+1*1), which is equal to Math.sqrt(2). But you already have a precalculated Math.SQRT2 constant, so you don't have to compute that. After calculating the vector length, you have to divide each of the vector's components by the length, and you get your facing vector
boxB.dx = 1 / Math.SQRT2;
boxB.dy = 1 / Math.SQRT2;

SO, to fix it, you just have to replace the box initialization lines with these:
boxA.xw = boxA._width / 2;
boxA.yw = boxA._height / 2;
boxA.dx = 1;
boxA.dy = 0;
boxB.xw = boxB._width / 2;
boxB.yw = boxB._height / 2;
boxB.dx = 1 / Math.SQRT2;
boxB.dy = 1 / Math.SQRT2;
boxB._rotation = Math.atan2 (boxB.dy, boxB.dx) * 180 / Math.PI;

In these lines, I use a little trigonometry (the Math.atan2() function call) so you can see how the box looks rotated.

Later, if you want, you can make things a bit more dynamic, like I did in the attached files.
Have fun!

TS

#86109 6 2005-07-25T23:05:51Z shoftee
(Yes, I know I'm double posting)

OK... let's have a look...
Whoa! This is very wrong!
I found your mistake, btw.
NEVER, AND I MEAN NEVER, declare a function in another function!!!
Also, you seem to have just put the whole thing into one big function!
The problem is that you even put the initialization in the function!

Fixed code:

boxA.px = boxA._x;
boxA.py = boxA._y;
boxA.xw = boxA._width / 2;
boxA.yw = boxA._height / 2;
boxA.dx = 1;
boxA.dy = 0;
boxB.px = boxB._x;
boxB.py = boxB._y;
boxB.xw = boxB._width / 2;
boxB.yw = boxB._height / 2;
boxB.dx = 1 / Math.SQRT2;
boxB.dy = 1 / Math.SQRT2;
boxB._rotation = Math.atan2 (boxB.dy, boxB.dx) * 180 / Math.PI;
// ---------------------------------------------------------
function ProjectBox (box, ax, ay) {
	ix = box.xw * box.dx;
	iy = box.xw * box.dy;
	jx = box.yw * -box.dy;
	jy = box.yw * box.dx;
	dpi = ix * ax + iy * ay;
	dpj = jx * ax + jy * ay;
	bsize = Math.abs (dpi) + Math.abs (dpj); // projection sur l'axe
	return bsize;
}
function collisionTest () {
	boxA.px = boxA._x;
	boxA.py = boxA._y;
	boxB.px = boxB._x;
	boxB.py = boxB._y;
	// ---------------------------------------------------------
	deltax = boxA.px - boxB.px; // vecteur entre les
	deltay = boxA.py - boxB.py; // 2 centres
	// ---------------------------------------------------------
	axis_x = boxA.dx; // vecteurs unitaires 
	axis_y = boxA.dy; // de la BoxA
	asize = boxA.xw; // rayon de la BoxA
	bsize = ProjectBox (boxB, axis_x, axis_y); // rayon de la BoxB
	dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
	penAx = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
	if (penAx > 0) {
 axis_x = boxB.dx;
 axis_y = boxB.dy;
 asize = ProjectBox (boxA, axis_x, axis_y);
 bsize = boxB.xw;
 dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
 penBx = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
 if (penBx > 0) {
 	axis_x = -boxA.dy;
 	axis_y = boxA.dx;
 	asize = boxA.yw;
 	bsize = ProjectBox (boxB, axis_x, axis_y);
 	dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
 	penAy = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
 	if (penAy > 0) {
  axis_x = -boxB.dy;
  axis_y = boxB.dx;
  asize = ProjectBox (boxA, axis_x, axis_y);
  bsize = boxB.yw;
  dsize = Math.abs (deltax * axis_x + deltay * axis_y);
  penBy = (asize + bsize) - dsize;
  if (penBy > 0) {
  	trace ("COLLISION");
  } else {
  	trace ("-4");
  }
 	} else {
  trace ("-3");
 	}
 } else {
 	trace ("-2");
 }
	} else {
 trace ("-1");
	}
}
setInterval (collisionTest, 200);

That, or the attached file.

#86178 2 2005-07-26T12:13:12Z shoftee
To know which object to project out of the other, you should first think which object is supposed to move, and which not. Obviously, if the object that's supposed to move(or also called a dynamic object) will have to be projected out of the other object.
Of course, that's the idea if only one object is dynamic. If both objects are dynamic, then you'll have to move each object by a certain amount. That amount depends on the mass of the objects. To fix this problem... you need to know the mass of each object. If object A has mass M and object B has mass N, and you have a penetration vector with length plen, then you have:

lenA = (M/M+N) * plen; // projection length for shape A
lenB = (N/M+N) * plen; // projection length for shape B

I think that's what you were looking for.

You should know that I haven't tested this, but I'm pretty sure it's correct.

TS

#3839 1 2005-07-27T17:58:41Z shoftee
OK. Here's the idea:
  • Invert Selection:
    All of the selected tiles are replaced with their opposites.
    In other words: The full parts become empty, and the empty - full.
  • Flip Selection:
    All of the selected tiles are flipped around their x and/or y axes. Flipping around both axes is the same as rotating 180 degrees.
  • Rotate Selection:
    ... obvious I think...
  • Transparency:
    OK, this is the one thing that I want in Ned more than anything else. With this little option turned on, when you move/copy the selection, only the non-empty tiles are moved/copied. This is VERY, and I mean VERY, useful when you want to put large shapes close to each other. I've needed this so many times that I've lost the count!
TS

#87423 10 2005-08-01T10:29:06Z shoftee
LOL
@Raigan:
Sorry for confusing you. boxB is actually a triangle. It's just that the name is boxB .
I know that this might be a little different from your code. I just project both shapes on the hypotenuse's normal. I know I could have calculated it instead of using projection, but I didn't want to confuse Bobo more than I might have .

@Bobo:
This might not be working because you don't use such a triangle as I expected you to use when I wrote the code. My code doesn't actually work for all right triangles, it works only for isosceles. If you want to test for all kinds of right triangles, then you should use your original idea:
Just replace these lines:
axis_x = Math.SQRT2 / 2;
axis_y = -Math.SQRT2 / 2;
With these:
var len = Math.sqrt( Math.pow( ax, 2 ) + Math.pow( ay, 2 ) );
ax /= len;
ay /= len;
This should make the collision testing work for all kinds of right triangles.
And sorry for this little mix up, It was my fault.

TS

#91595 19 2005-08-16T11:41:19Z shoftee
LOL. The thing is that when you set the particle's mass to 0, the relaxation doesn't move it, because when it multiplies invmass by something and invmass is zero, the overall result will be zero, so the velocity doesn't change.
So the idea is to make a new Boolean property for each particle, let's name it, "dragged" and when you press on a particle you set this dragged property to true and set the particle's mass to 0. When you release it, you set it back to false.
Then in your relaxation function, you make an if-else statement, so the relaxation is in the "else". Instead of "relaxing" it, while the mouse is down, you set the x and y coordinates of the dragged particle to _root._xmouse and _root._ymouse accordingly.
I think that should work, although I haven't tested it.
Hope that helps.

TS

#93200 16 2005-08-22T15:23:26Z shoftee
You can do that in just 3 lines:
onClipEvent(enterFrame) {
this._x += (Key.isDown(Key.RIGHT) - Key.isDown(Key.LEFT)) * 2;
}

If you're pressing right, the result is 2:
(1 - 0) * 2 = 2
And if you're pressing left, the result is -2:
(0 - 1) * 2 = -2
If you're pressing both, the result is 0:
(1 - 1) * 2 = 0



TS

Just in case you didn't know

#101016 18 2005-09-16T18:49:12Z shoftee
Edit: Sorry, I pressed the Add Reply button by accident... typing reply now

Since it's about MY code, I guess I should answer it, and I will.
...
len = Math.sqrt (deltax * deltax + deltay * deltay);
boxB.dx = deltax / len;
boxB.dy = deltay / len;
boxB._rotation = Math.atan2 (boxB.dy, boxB.dx) * 180 / Math.PI;
...

We have the facing vector (deltax, deltay) .
For the code to work properly, you have to normalize the facing vector. To normalize a vector means to make it 1 unit long. And this is exactly what the first 3 lines do.
If you want 45 degrees rotation, your facing vector has to be (1, 1). (I think )
So, if you want to normalize ANY vector, you have to calculate it's length, and then divide each vector component (x and y) by the length.
So for the vector (1, 1), you have a length of about 1.41 or something of the sort. Then you divide deltax and deltay by len and you get the normalized vector (dx, dy).
The fourth line just rotates the box so you see it rotated. If you remove the last line, both boxes will have 0 degrees rotation, but boxA will still collide with a box that's with 45 degrees rotation.

And the last part of the last line converts the radians returned by atan2 to degrees.

1 radian = 180 / PI degrees.
So to convert to degrees, we just multiply the radians by 180 / Math.PI .

Quote: LITlle, 2005-09-15T00:18:29Z
... In this thread I saw an example with rotation, but I'd like to study an example with for example a sloping static (not 45 degree) line and a square.
If you want a line, just make the half-width/half-height of one of the squares 0. It's as simple as that.

TS
#101231 20 2005-09-17T17:50:59Z shoftee
I knew you would ask this!
By now, I told you how to get a rotation from a facing vector. Now I'll tell you how to get a facing vector from rotation.
You have to use sin and cos.

deltax = Math.cos(x * Math.PI / 180);
deltay = Math.sin(x * Math.PI / 180);

, where x is your rotation in degrees.
The vector (deltax, deltay) should now be unit-length, so you woudn't need any of the length calculations. Which makes the boxB initialization lines:

x = 35;
deltax = Math.cos(x * Math.PI / 180);
deltay = Math.sin(x * Math.PI / 180);
boxB.dx = deltax;
boxB.dy = deltay;
boxB._rotation = Math.atan2 (boxB.dy, boxB.dx) * 180 / Math.PI;

*tests code*
Ah, it works .
Hope that helps.

TS

#115304 18 2005-11-15T23:27:54Z shoftee
Quote: Sweep, 2005-11-14T17:09:38Z
(...) and ultimately shouldn't be too hard to code.

One thing: don't count on it.

Even though it does make sense, you won't have to put mines and everything, I still don't see it happening. First, because I don't think it'd be THAT useful, and second, because it definitely WON'T be easy to code:
More tiles = need more RAM.
More tiles = loads longer.
More tiles = a whole lot of stuff to be rewrited, making me wonder if it's even worth it.

That's what I have to say

EDIT: Actually, I have even more stuff to say:

*points at Metanet tutorial 2*
You have to have a border. It's the way the collision engine works. The ninja SHOULDN'T go outside the level, that's what the border was made for. It shouldn't even be NEAR the level border, because the tile the ninja is in should ALWAYS have exactly 9 neighbouring tiles. That or the collisions f*ck up.
And, finally, I'm pretty sure this suggestion won't be implemented soon.

(Feel free to correct me if you think I've made some mistake)

#122026 5 2005-12-11T06:57:18Z shoftee
OK, after reading both posts, I can now say what your problem is.
In the picture you posted, you seem to just take the overlapMid and totalMid "y" components, which isn't what you're supposed to do.
What you're supposed to do is to project those two points on the normal (not on the global "y" axis) and then see how they're positioned. As far as I can imagine, that should work.

I hope that helps.

#131451 2 2006-01-10T20:46:29Z shoftee
This _would_ add a little more fun to the whole death thing. But actually measuring how much distance you flew, or jumped, or speed (...well, maybe not speed, speed is easy...) will be slowass stuff.
Furthermore, according to the particle system that controls the rag-doll, there's no way of measuring how much distance the ragdoll moved, since it's not actually a whole object, so it doesn't have a "center" which you measure the distance with. Same goes for body-parts.

So... NO!


TS
#131454 4 2006-01-10T21:01:26Z shoftee

#131471 6 2006-01-10T23:18:09Z shoftee
LOL. I suppose it seemed like a really dumb question, but to me(since I'm a programmer I guess ), distance isn't a clearly defined thing. It might mean the shortest distance between two, but it might also mean the length of the trajectory of an object. You see, there's a big difference in how you calculate those. But, I suppose you mean the latter (the trajectory length). I don't really know how this could be done fast, but, IMHO, it's not worth the time .

Sorry

#132565 7 2006-01-15T07:57:33Z shoftee
I am obviously the only one here (except M&R, of course) who can actually say what pain it'll be to implement Undo. Generally, undo needs a lot of memory. That, if you aske me, will be the biggest drawback. Of course, if we're talking about something like 3-10 undo levels, not much of a problem, but if you want 50-100, well you're stupid to make such a huge mistake in the beginning.
I think it's a nice idea actually, it MIGHT help, but on the other hand, when you get used to ned you won't make such dumb mistakes, so there should be an option to turn it off or something (for the sake of free memory). So... yeah .

#133277 13 2006-01-18T00:07:01Z shoftee
OK... Even though I have beaten N, I don't consider myself a pro... I'm not that brag-ish yet .
Anywho, the key to most levels is to keep trying one route over and over. If you screw up somewhere, you try it again and hopefully you won't screw up in the same place.
There is NOT a specific way to avoid gausses. Even if you somehow memorize a way to avoid a gauss in a specific level, there's a really big chance of screwing something up (by that I mean, holding a key for 1/40th of the second longer than needed). Same goes for drones and thwumps.
As for me, I trust my reaction time (mostly).

#156653 5 2006-03-22T08:01:08Z shoftee
It's really easy.
Each frame, you take the getTimer() and subtract the getTimer() from the previous frame. That's how you calculate how much MS it took to draw a frame. That's all there is to it.

Actually... LouDog was right all along. You guys DO suck at maths.

Let's analyze this one:

A map has a 5 after 5 votes...
Someone snipes it cuz he's a jealous n00b.
(5 * 5 + 0) / 6 = 25 / 6 = 4.1(6)
Some one 5nipes it in return...
(4.1(6) * 6 + 5) / 7 = 30 / 7 = 4.(285714)

Where's the balance in that? Did it return to a 5? NO!
Geez, you guys are so dumbassful.

Just give up, you won't fix anything with this. You'll just make it even worse. Really.

#9560 1 2006-06-18T21:31:31Z shoftee
OMFG! A SUGGESTION! BY ME?! IT CAN'T BE!
[/caps]

Has it ever happened to you? You're peacefully playing the game... when suddenly, you're ninja goes through the mouse cursor, and hits a mine which happened to be behind it. Then you're all raging etc. There's a simple solution actually.
You could just hide the cursor while playing like so:
Mouse.hide();
And when you're not playing (Example: on the main menu)
Mouse.show();
Simple enough, eh?

Now for an expert opinion:
Diagonal thwumps will never be implemented, simply because if you rotate a thwump by 45 it's bound box becomes larger than a tile, thus the collision engine will be unable to process it properly . Even _IF_ we scale them down to proper size, it'll still be a big pain to implement the moving-object 45 collision. A pretty good idea though, too bad it won't happen

#201842 3 2006-07-12T20:41:14Z shoftee
Well, it's kinda hard to answer, because I'm lazy, but here goes.

I'd suppose they used AS 1.0 objects simply because they were in a hurry. But I'd have to leave the details to Mare and Raigan themselves .
About the other stuff...
While the ninja is alive, it's simply a circle (collision-wise). Of course, they still keep track of where the body-part positions are. Each limb consists of three particles, and IK is used to connect em properly. When the ninja dies, they replace the "circle" movie clip with the particles, which make the actual ragdoll that you see. If the death is "exploding" then they disconnect the particles, and make new ones where needed (like at the elbow or knee). Clear enough?
You also might want to check out Jakobsen's article on verlet physics, if you haven't already. That could help you quite a bit if you're interested in ragdolls.
And about your little project: well, it's pretty good for a ten-day-old project . Keep it up

#210307 3 2006-08-02T08:12:29Z shoftee
Well, the way pokemaniac said would work, but it's kinda harder to understand...

You know the main Ned screen, right? Well you copy the level data and put it into the 'level data text' field in Ned, then you load the level with [L] (after clicking outside the text field to prevent NaNs), then you copy the demo you want to see and put it in the 'demo data' text field, and then you load the demo with [1] (again after clicking outside the field to prevent it from screwing up) and you play it with [Q]...

That should work

#10544 1 2006-08-09T09:44:11Z shoftee
Well, quite some time has passed since the map-pack idea came to me, so I think it's safe to say that this map pack wasn't meant to be a "legacy clone". It is true, however, that Legacy had an incredible influence on my map-making ability. I'd like to start an endless rant about what's the difference between a column and a bunch of episodes (and also the difference between an episode and a bunch of maps), but in the end I decided to make the map-pack speak for itself.
If I had to say the truth, I'm really proud of this map pack, and I feel I found my own style of map-making while I made the maps in it.
Now, onto the more important part. While I was making this map pack, I suddenly realised that I've never made a single dedication map. And since I'm such a nice guy, I decided to make, not one, not two, not THREE, but f*cking thirteen of 'em. Yes, thirteen. And, another idea that came to my mind while I was at it. Since these deds are for a few really special people, I decided to make a pretty interesting experiment. I decided, to carefully examine each of the map-making styles of twelve of those thirteen people, merge 'em with my own, and see what comes out. I should say, the results were astounding.

tktktk - Great, great, GREAT maps in the legacy. You absolutely deserve this one.

Kablizzy - Your maps were incredible too, man. Simply awesome. Keep up the awesomeness.

maximo - I think this one doesn't need to be explained. It's like... maximo. It'd be hard to find a reason NOT to make him a dedication.

nevermore - One of the best, what more is there to say?

LittleViking - As our most active admin, an NTTS first place winner, a good OP in the IRC channel, and also a great guy with great map-making ability, this one is totally justified.

Sweep - Thank you, Sweep, for bitesizeNUMA, the Dronies, and all of the other little (and big) things you've done for this community.

(after listing the legacy members, and no, I haven't forgotten about Ian and Lucidium, but I'd rather see their maps in Expanse too before doing them dedications)

KinGAleX - For the helpful comments and advice when I was in a bad mood, and the great judging in the NTTS, you totally deserve this.

spect - A good friend, a great highscorer, even after retiring from it all, and has huge potential for map-making. Also, he ran the NTTS superbly. Again, totally justified.

krusch - An astounding map-maker, a good judge, and overall a really friendly guy.

blue_tetris - A great guy, with lots of heart and talent, great story-teller, great map-maker (yes, I'm talking about MTI and stuff), and his dedication map is the easiest to recognize.

Barabajagal - Again, a great guy, an awesome map-maker, and most likely the future first place winner on NedCon 7.

Mare and Raigan - Last, but absolutely not least (don't you even dare think about it), I made a dedication to the creators of this game we all know and love. Great job guys, simply awesome. Keep it up
  • Dave - The One (guessed by thecalcgenius).
    Like I said, it's the most obvious of them all. You seem to have been thinking too much about this one. The tileset is made out of... like... tetris pieces. It's hella obvious!
  • Mare and Raigan - m.mirror.r (again, by thecalcgenius).
    Apart from the obvious title, nobody recognised the '&' sign in the tileset. In some fonts it looks like a handwritten capital E, but with two lines going up and down from it. Nevermind, this is the second most easiest, the next are gonna be harder... I hope...
  • LittleViking - Quicksort (guessed by spect).
    I really liked how this map turned out. I've always seen the LV map as a map with platforms, seeker drones and mines. Don't ask me why, I wouldn't know . Anyways, nice guess spect, really nice. Also, the way the drones go in different direction when the hit the opposite wall is so... quicksort-ish. (programmer reference, don't try understanding it).
  • spect - Vertical is for n00bs. (OMFG! spect guessed his own ded! Awesome!)
    This map, I think, is pretty metanetty. But spect's style (at least from the maps I've seen) is also pretty metanetty. So there .
  • krusch - "MUST. EAT. BRAINS." said the fluffy bunny (Again, by spect)
    Here I left the style of the map to speak for itself. Most people thought this map would be Blizzy's because of the name, but no, it's not. It's good ol' krusch's map, and a good one at that, too. The gold and mine placement are mine, at least I think so . Also, if you look at it closely, the bottom-center part of the map, that's a bunny with flappy ears, viewed from the back. The top-center part is the same bunny, but upside down, and faced from the front. And that half-tile is it's face expression. A cold, half-tile face expression... . Nice guess again, spect!
  • nevermore - "'Tis the wind < 3 > nothing < 4 >." (by bobodahobo)
    Back into the chamber turning, all my soul within me burning,
    Soon again I heard a tapping, something louder than before,
    "Surely," said I, "surely, that is something at my window lattice.
    Let me see, then, what thereat is, and this mystery explore.
    Let my heart be still a moment, and this mystery explore.
    " 'Tis the wind, and nothing more."
    Obvious enough?
  • KinGAleX - The desert ain't THAT scarce. This is. (by bobodahobo, again)
    A reference to a KinGAleX map. Yep. And cute mine-patterns too. Totally. Wewt.
  • tktktk - Modern (by spect. Nice work spect!)
    I really like the tileset and the action in this map... I don't know about you, but it seemed tktktk-ish enough
  • Kablizzy - Grablic (OMG! spect again!)
    Inspired by Legacy 49-0 - "Flatline". The name is a hint that comes from Blizzy's nedcon entry. For some reason the... uhhmmm empty... space... things... whatever, in the tileset reminded me of grabling hooks... I'm weird like that, nevermind. The way the drones block your path in this map is really interesting.
  • Barabajagal - "Sorrow more distant than a star." (spect?! Again?!)
    This one really made me think about map-making styles. From the maps Baraba has on NUMA now, I could say that he loves using floorguards, and his mine pattern is quite artsy... It came out as a pretty cool map, and it's probably the hardest of the deds. Most of you probably don't know, but "Barabajagal" is actually a name of a song. The name of this map is a line from the lyrics and somehow it really suited the map... No idea how that happened, really.
  • maximo - Destruction Art (OMG CYBY!)
    Heh. I like this map. A bit disappointed that maximo didn't like it as much though. It was inspired by 66-2 of Legacy, named "I don't wanna go to town". I've said it before, I'll say it again, me and maximo both agreed that episode 66 was hell, and I think all of the Legacy Elite would agree with that, too. And the name, the tileset reminded me of one of the "Destruction Arts" in Bleach. Too bad only a few would get the idea. Right...
  • Sweep - Ponies can be decieving. (OMG CYBY AGAINZOR!)
    Now this, is a bit illogical. When I looked through Sweep's maps on NUMA, I realised that he had no defined style... which was a kind of a shock to me, seeing as he has made maps for a long long while. Still, I remember one of his old maps called "Sun" (nice map, btw), so I decided to make something with drones going around the level... in the end I decided to make it all square-y, and make the drones go around the squares... It turned out nicely, I should say. One thing that I realised afterwards is this: "OMG! I forgot to put squares in the lower left corner! I screwed up!" and then I look up: "Phew, I forgot to put squares in the top right corner, too.. all is well...".
Right... so after a long while, you finally managed to guess all of em. Nice job, to all of you. And since TCG and bobodahobo helped out, I'ma make them deds too, I guess. And to the rest of you: That's what you get for not wanting to play a game as simple as this .

And the thirteenth is:
  • cyby (yes, I'm writing it as cyby on purpose) - ** x 4 (HA!)
    So... why cyby? Well, why not, goddammit! This guy, you guys have no idea how much he helped me with this thing. I still remember how we both double- and triple-checked the maps for fun-ness and stuff in the early hours of the morning. As my totally-worthy-of-being arch-nemesis, cyby totally deserves this ded! Right... so what's with the name? It's a bit subtle, but it's still not impossible to see. There are the letters C and J in the map. C made by the bounce blocks, and J from the gold and bounce blocks. Four times each, so there you have it: CJ x 4. Right...
SO... is that all I can make? Certainly not. In fact, you guys should actually expect another bunch o' levels a bit after Expanse (I'm about half way there already ). Again, I'd like to thank The Legacy Team for making such a great pack, KA for the helpful advice, spect for being such and nice guy, same for krusch, Baraba for helping me with the maps, Dave for all of the great MTI clones he made, cyby for the help with the all-golding and overall playtesting, and last, but not least at all, Mare and Raigan for making this wonderful game. Keep on rocking guys, you're good at it.

#219240 15 2006-08-24T18:46:25Z shoftee
Let's just say it might not be as easy as you think. (As I have already said in the thread mascote_ponto linked to).

[RANT]

Quote: thecalcgenius in that thread
Yeah, it seems (to my unworthy eyes, compared to M&R) that it would be easy to show. Since numbers are used to correspond to keypresses, perhaps we could signal -1 to be the suicide command.

Ex:
50:35791394|35791394|243|0|0|-1
would make the ninja move a ways to the right, stop, and kill himself.

Okay then, I may as well explain it...
First off, for 50 frames, it would look more like...
50:35791394|35791394|243|0|0|0|0|0
Why? Because... the numbers between the seperators are 7 frames of demo data. And since the number there is 50 (which means 51 frames, since we start the count from 0), that means the first 7 numbers are for the first 49 frames, and there's one more for the other two frames. After clearing that up, let's see what those numbers actually mean... And I'll also explain why what tcg suggested wouldn't work.

Note: if you don't understand binary, you might as well give up on reading this.

As of now, each frame can be represented as a number from 0 to 7 (inclusive). So that's from 000 to 111 in binary. Those digits describe what keys are pressed. The way the current demo data is, the right digit means you're pressing the [left] key, the middle is the [right] key, and the left is the [jump] key. For example, if we have the frame [101] it means we're pressing the [jump] key and the [left] key. Simple enough?
Okay, so how do we have one number for seven frames? Well, we just have seven bunches of 3 digits (in binary), for example [010], [010], [010], [010], [010], [010], [010] (which is pressing the [right] key for seven frames), we just make one big binary number from these.
Which would be: [010010010010010010010010010] (Note that the first of those frames is the rightmost three digits)
Converted to decimal, this is the number 35791394.
After explaining that, I'll now explain why it's gonna be troublesome to implement the [kill] key thing.
Since we have to put another key in each frame, that means we now have bunches of FOUR digits, for example [1000] means you're pressing just the [kill] key. Yes, but what do we do with existing demos then? All of the demos up until now are made for three keys, so if we add another binary digit to each frame, it's obvious why they wouldn't work.
The number above, [010010010010010010010010010] would mean that we go to the right on the first frame (the [0010] in the right) and then pressing left and committing suicide on the second (the [1001] on the left of the [0010] frame). Thus, as far as we know, implementing this would break all of the demos we have until now.
And that thing TCG suggested: for one, no negative numbers allowed, the way negative numbers are described in binary makes it impossible for this to work in all cases.

[/RANT]

Still it bothers me because it's a good suggestion that I'd like to see implemented, too... Ah well...

Also, it's pretty much the simplest and easiest way to compress this kind of data. Binary FTW!

EDIT: Furthermore, this can be easily discovered (at least for me) if you just play around with the keys and the demo data in frame-by-frame mode.

#229386 4 2006-09-16T18:28:24Z shoftee
Orion.
That Comrade+1984= Big Brother map... it won't appear in the list.
Firstly, because you forgot the $ before the map data, and secondly, because it has '+' and '=' in the name, which screw up the data since they are special chars. You'll have to use their % codes.
*checks*
%2B for '+' and...
%3D for '='

#13077 1 2006-12-22T21:31:42Z shoftee
Note: Fimision is stubborn about not believing this. That's partially the reason for me to post it here

Do you guys know that 0.(9) actually equals 1?

A way to prove it (there are many):

Let x = 0.(9)
We multiply both sides by 10, which makes:
10x = 9.(9)
9.(9) = 9 + 0.(9) = 9 + x
10x = 9 + x
9x = 9
x = 1

=> 0.(9) = 1

Honestly, the people who actually know this are so few it's not even funny!

That's pretty easy to explain - it checks which penetration vector is smaller, and uses that (as you already said). For most cases that works perfectly, but not for this one. Try it and tell us if it works.

#13255 1 2007-01-01T05:00:32Z shoftee
Well, well, isn't it me again, posting more maps.
What can I say... it's been a while since I last did this, and frankly, it's only my second pack. Yet... it's probably my last one. Yes, 2 columns is enough work for me.

This past year... was my best, and at the same time, my worst year yet. Where did it all go, I ask myself... I can't really answer that. I had my first girlfriend, my first kiss... my first break-up... and, to tell you the truth, it was quite the experience.
And, no, this time I'm not going to skip the rant.

I will, however, make it tiny.
[RANT]

Where did that last year go...
As I'm typing this right now, it's 00:07 on January 1st 2007. And, frankly, I only feel older. I feel like another year of my life passed. It's all over. I had her love, and I lost it. Ahh... the good times we can't repeat, is it?

[The Offspring - Can't Repeat]

I love this song. It's one of the few songs that describe my feelings so well.

I met her on 3rd of March. She confessed to me around May. My first kiss was on 14th of July. And our break-up was around the beginning of September.

I'd like to share a few things I learnt in this past year.

Don't make women wait for love. They suck at being patient.

I learnt that I can be alive, that I can be happy.
I learnt that I'm right here, right now, to trust not only what I think, but also what I feel.
I learnt that life isn't so bad if you have someone to spend it with.
I learnt that even someone like me, a geeky computer game player, INTP, can find that special someone in this vast world of endless possibilities.
I learnt that even I could be optimistic if I had the support.
I learnt a lot of things, yes.
I learnt what love is, at the same time, I learnt what real pain is.
I also found out that my tolerance against certain substances isn't that developed.
And by "certain" substances I don't mean just alcohol. (In fact, I have yet to get drunk. I just realized that I get a bit excited even after a little drink.)
I learnt that I would hardly be able to stop myself from doing certain things when I'm in the mood. I'm sure most of you should be able to understand that.


A thought:
"All I have is this one moment. What I'm feeling right now is my only truth, my only proof. Everything else might be just my mind playing tricks on me.
Yes, this one moment. Everything before it is the past which only bothers me. Everything after it is the unknown future, which only frightens me."

There are quite a few things I'd like to mention. For example, one of Blizzy's posts in The Real N!... http://therealn.com/index.php?comic=10

Indeed, some days, I feel happy, other days - sad.
Some days, I feel like I still have a chance, like there's still hope, other days - like everything's gone, like everything's over.
Some days, I feel like going out in the world and yelling "You can't control me!", other days I admit total defeat to the unbreakable logic that binds us.
It's sad how I have to go through this, knowing it's going to be over in a few years. It's really sad that I know these things.


A quote:
"Ignorance is bliss."

Do you hate that quote? You do? Do you wanna know why you hate it? Because it's the effin' truth. Ignorance is such bliss! How I wish, how I really wish... that I never knew the stuff I do. I wish I didn't know how simple of a thing love really is, because it's so simple we don't have control over it at all. The feeling of knowing, and not being able to do anything about it, it's enough to make you go insane. It's like watching your life on a screen - all the good moments pass you by, and you have no hands with which to grab onto them. All the bad moments pass you by, and you have no hands to make em move faster. It's ironic.


"Live your life to the fullest, for you have only one shot at it."

Yes, I think I've finally managed to let go. Of course, it might be just one of my "high" phases again, but I feel like I want to be alive. After what happened, I now have a new view of the world. I can see that all I really need is my life, because you can be happy as long as you have that.
To all of you who are still in doubt, stop thinking about it, and actually do something. It worked for me.

[/RANT]
If you're feeling suicidal enough to actually read it, copy the stuff in the RANT tags and paste it into Notepad, or something.

And.. an endless rant it was, no?

...

Apart from the number of dedications I've done this time, I dedicate this whole pack to...
Time and change. Yeah, those two things might be able to describe the pack.

Now... onto the important stuff...
I will divide this pack in... four parts:
1. The Column.
2. Scrapped levels.
3. Uncut levels.
4. Special levels.

1. The Column.
I hardly give random map names to my maps. Almost all of them are references to different things, some not even related to the map itself... but still!
List of references
A lot of the deds are in the column... the map names make it obvious, in fact.

2. Scrapped levels.
Generally levels which I wasn't really satisfied with. I still left 'em in there, just in case I found 'em good for something along the way. Apparently, I didn't.

3. Uncut levels.
Levels which I couldn't find a place somewhere else in the pack go here. Yeah...

4. Special levels.
I still find the Special levels to be the best ones in the pack.
  • "Goddamn it!"
    A reference to the number 777... duh...
  • "Pushover"
    My first and only joint map. Tileset by BuddyLee.
  • "No time to platformulate"
    A dedication to Platformula (86-4) by Sendy.
  • "Transfer"
    A dedication to Valves by Lucidium.
  • "Total Ripoff"
    This map is special. It's an obvious total ripoff of a LouDog004 map, more specifically, "Longest", 82-2 in the game. I talked with LouDog and he gave me permission to release it, but I still feel as if my intention would be totally misunderstood.
    I admit it, I ripped off a map and I'm releasing it. And I'm doing it exactly because I've already done it with a Sendy map. Most of you don't even know about that thing, and that's why this is aimed at the ones that DO know.
    I've grown up a bit since then, y'hear? You probably don't even care about that anymore, but it so happens that I do.
    A long time ago when I started making maps, I couldn't help but play other people's maps and kinda follow the same style as the top-rated authors. I sucked at it, so nobody even noticed. All of the mini-maps in my last pack were actually inspired either by the other mini-maps in the pack or MasterJmd's maps (who later turned out to be AMLT). After some time, a few things happened (refer to the RANT if you're really interested), and everything changed. I started making maps for fun, and all of em were awesome. I started to feel something new, something different coming...
    Oh damn, I started ranting again, didn't I?... I better stop... (In fact, I think what I already said made my point obvious.)
  • "Hope says hi."
    A dedication to my pseudo-alter-ego. He greets you all.
  • "Leveling"
    A dedication to object placement. I liked this one.
  • "Backtrack"
    ... DFS FTW!
  • "Surface"
    I liked this one, too.
  • "Night of flight"
    I dedicate this map to IRC. I like it a lot.
  • "Fractured (Fell of my bike)"
    A dedication to Steve from Vent. He will never see this thread, but he knows we all love him
  • "It's snowing gold outside!"
    The dedication I promised to thecalcgenius for his help with the last pack.
  • "b0b0d4h0b0"
    A ded to bobodahobo, same reason as tcg.
  • "AYBABTU reference"
    For guessing the reference in 10-2. Also helped out with playtesting, which I'm eternally grateful for.
  • "Phun, ain't it?"
    A dedication to rocket_thumped for helping out with playtesting. I like the map.
It was fun guys, it really was.

[NEW YEAR ADD TO THE POST]

April 2005th when I first met these great guys.
May 2005th when I had my 15th birthday.
June 2005th when I pwnd my English exams.
July 2005th when I was having fun playing N.

January 2007th. I suck at N and everything around it.

#305865 4 2007-03-17T17:30:20Z shoftee
They have it set to max frame-rate (e.g 120 fps) - 8.(3) ms/f

The main problem in N (which is also the cause for the slow-downs) is screen redraw... The scripts account for about 10% of all CPU usage... So it doesn't actually slow down, but just "skips" drawing some frames... It should run at the same speed, otherwise.
Anyways, Flash handles that stuff. You can be sure that even if it doesn't really show it instantly, it detects the key-presses.

Though, I'm not really sure about that, I just think that it makes sense.

You could try setInterval() if you're really picky about the rate. You just make a function which checks for key-presses and call it every I-don't-know-how-many milliseconds. Check it up in the reference.

#312815 3 2007-04-13T18:12:52Z shoftee
Fol67... I... I don't understand how your brain works... He wasn't asking what the code meant. He said he has problems with projection... C/C++ and ActionScript (which is based on Java/Javascript) use pretty much the same syntax. He shouldn't have any problem with understanding it...

Anyways, welcome to Metanet Forums.

I myself used to know a lot about this projection stuff, but it seems I've forgotten most of it... I haven't helped anyone with it in a long, long time. ^.^;

I think I know what your problem is (after having to read part of the tutorial again)
Quote: The tutorial page
The formula for projecting vector a onto vector b is:

proj.x = ( dp / (b.x*b.x + b.y*b.y) ) * b.x;
proj.y = ( dp / (b.x*b.x + b.y*b.y) ) * b.y;

where dp is the dotprod of a and b: dp = (a.x*b.x + a.y*b.y)

Note that the result is a vector; also, (b.x*b.x + b.y*b.y) is simply the length of b squared.

If b is a unit vector, (b.x*b.x + b.y*b.y) = 1, and thus a projected onto b reduces to:

proj.x = dp*b.x;
proj.y = dp*b.y;
My assumption would be that you don't normalize the vector, so it just multiplies the acceleration a lot. You normalize the vector by dividing both its x and y to its length.
[smartass]
That's easy to prove: if you have the vector (x;y), with a length of ln = √(x*x+y*y), then the new vector would have ln2 = √ ((x*x)/(ln*ln) + (y*y)/(ln*ln)) = √((x*x + y*y)/(ln*ln) = √ (x*x+y*y)/ln = ln/ln = 1...
[/smartass]
Uhm... so yeah... try that.

Next time you encounter a problem, don't be afraid of simply posting your code in C. I doubt anyone here who could help you wouldn't understand it.

Hope that helps.

#313463 6 2007-04-15T18:30:10Z shoftee
Hrm. For starters, you don't project points on lines, you project vectors on vectors. I think that at some point you mixed those three up and it all went downhill from there.

To check if a circle is colliding with a line and with a segment are different things. If you're checking for a line, all you have to do is check the distance between the line and the circle...
(In an attempt to make my explanation clearer) I made a few diagrams:

This explains why (in your case) it's not just one simple check that needs to be done. These are three possible positions of the ball and the segment you're checking for collisions with (the fourth (and last) case is the one you had in mind). The red lines are what you are trying to calculate, the green ones are what you should be trying to calculate. (Red + Green = Brown)



The process of projecting a vector on another vector. The result is also a vector. I will use the same colors for the next few diagrams, too. (This diagram is actually just to show you what colors I'm using, though. The next few are the useful ones.)



When taking the point (x,y), you're actually taking the vector (0,0)->(x,y) and trying to project it on the vector (x1,y1)->(x2,y2). That's where your mistake is, too. What you need to do is take the vector (x1,y1)->(x,y) and project it on the line (x1,y1)->(x2,y2).




These two are essentially the same. It's just that in the lower one, everything is shifted so that (x1,y1) becomes (0,0). Your mistake was that you mixed the two up.



This last one is the case where the collision point is in the end of the segment. In this case, what you need to check is if the resulting vector lies on the line, or outside the line.

I think that pretty much wraps it up.
Hope you understand it (even though I highly doubt it)...

#327719 4 2007-06-05T18:51:15Z shoftee
Nah, SAT works fine in 3D. It's just that you have to make a set of three 2D checks. I suggest you start with the simplest type: Just a full tile (in this case, a cube), then build off of that. I also suggest you make that piece code as flexible as possible, as it'll act as the base of the other parts.

EDIT: As a side note, some of these shapes aren't as easy to code as you make them look. More specifically, I mean the curved ones. I'm too busy to explain right now, but doing them is more complex then just adding up two checks. If you do it like this, shapes 5, 6, 8 and 9 will have an "edge" going from the top-left-back to the bottom-right-front vertexes. Look at your 45 degree 3D tiles and you should be able to see what I mean.
#327985 6 2007-06-06T11:33:05Z shoftee
Actually, you haven't included the sphere cut-out shapes in your graphic. The one you show here are a lot more complex, as you already confirmed.

Anyways, moving along to your problems.

The first thing you must understand is that you can "subtract" dimensions when doing these checks, in order to simplify them.
A sphere-plane check is a one-dimensional check (i.e 3D minus 2D) - you need a single distance value, a sphere-edge check is a two-dimensional check (3D minus 1D) - you need two distance values, and a sphere-point check is a three-dimensional check (3D minus 0D) - you need three distance values. Accordingly, a sphere-plane check is equivalent to a edge-point check, a sphere-edge check is equivalent to a plane-point check, and so on.

The box-cube check is pretty obvious, IMO, and you seem to have noticed that too... Now for the sphere-cube check. Skipping the sphere-plane and sphere-edge checks (which are pretty straight-forward and equivalents are already explained in the Metanet tutorials), we go right to the vertex VR case.
First of all, you need to know which the vertex closest to the sphere is... which is pretty simple, really. You take the delta between both centers (the sphere and the cube), and that'll result in three values. Then you take the sign of each component (represented with either +1 or -1) and multiply it by the cube's half-side. After that, you use the 3D Pythagoras theorem (d = x + y + z) to check the distance between the resulting point and the sphere's center. If you can wrap your mind around three dimensions (which I assume you can as you've undertaken such a challenge in the first place) things should go smoothly from there.

For the curved-ramp-box and curved-ramp-sphere, things are a bit more messy. Explaining exactly how to calculate the vectors would be a bit too time-consuming, but I could tell you the VRs you need to check. You have two full sides in the ramp (which makes 2 plane regions, 7 edge regions and 6 point regions) two 1/4 circle cut-outs (which are still easy to deal with, they're point-circle distance checks), two VRs for the curved edges (which are point-sphere distance checks), and the cylinder cut out - edge-sphere (which can be simplified to point-circle).
IMO, if you've managed to get the cube-sphere checks, the hardest part here are the curved edges.
The curved-ramp-box checks are easier - You don't have to worry about the curved edges as much. The hardest part here is the box-cylinder check (equivalent to a rectangle-circle check, which is already explained in the Metanet tutorials), so you should have relatively little problems with it.

That was, of course, the case for the curved ramps. The 45 degree ramps are a lot similar to those:
Again, two full sides (2 plane VRs, 7 edge VRs, 6 point VRs), two triangles (2 plane VRs), the edges on each side of the ramp (2 edge VRs) and the ramp itself (another plane VR)

The biggest problems come when you do the ramp-edge checks. The idea with them is like this: you have to check if the sphere is hitting an edge. That's a two-dimensional projection - one sphere-plane check for the ramp plane and one sphere-plane check for the plane with the triangle.

I think that wraps it up.

If you run into problems, I suggest you use pen and paper to clear things up. If that doesn't help, tell me and I'll try my best to make some diagrams for you.

Hope that helped,

TS

#342234 9 2007-07-12T21:34:56Z shoftee
*hits head*
Ah damn, should've seen this thread earlier, sorry 'bout that. I myself have no problem with you using my code. (Hell, you should be having problems with reading it anyways.)
The rotation is registered by the two values dx and dy. If you change those accordingly to the angle, it should work fine. The problem is that they're initialized in the enterFrame function, so it doesn't matter how much you change them since they switch back to parallel each frame. (Forgive my stupidity, as I rarely look through code once I get it working.)
If you want to get results, it's best that you take the initialization out of there. I'ma attach a working version.
*fixes a few miscellaneous bugs*
(This still uses trig for the rotation, though.)

Hope that helped.

EDIT: What you noted about projectBox() is true, but I'd rather not play around with low-level stuff like that. Changing the value itself is a lot easier to understand, don't you think?

Moving N wouldn't help at all. Firstly, you should get a program like CCleaner and use that to get rid of temporary files. (Make sure you back up your N data before you do that, the program has a history of wiping the shared objects that N uses.)

Generally, it's good to not keep useless stuff on it.

#18716 1 2008-01-05T11:43:44Z shoftee
Quote: TS on Zenith 59-4
If there is one thing I love more than impossible challenges, it is screaming orgasms. This level is my sex-dream come true!

I did it.
For a long time, I had been saying,
After Zenith, I stop playing N.
But, to be honest, I was doubting that statement even as I was passing through 59-3.

[Will it be enough?] I was thinking.
[Will I be able to do it?] I was thinking.

Even as I moved my mouse over the button in userlevels, I was still doubting that statement.

And, then, it appeared.

I said Yes, this is it, and pressed spacebar.

As I was dying again, again, and again, I was contemplating on all the time I had spent playing this game.

I think that this game not only taught me how to play it with skill. This game taught me a lot about life in general.

No matter how many times you jump, you will always fall, and break every bone in your body.
No matter how much you run, the floorguard will still catch up.
At first, it hurts, but it gets a lot better when you keep on doing it.



Ive played this game when I was happy.
Ive played it when I was sad.
Ive played it when I was bored, excited.
Ive even played it at 7 AM instead of sleeping calmly in my bed.

The time I had spent on this game, it was the best time of my life.

And, parting with it, is sad, but necessary.

You know, it kinda reminds me of first love, now that I think about it.

Yes, it might have been love.
The feeling I felt when I played it might have been love.

Ive spent more time playing this game than I have spent out with my pals.
Ive spent more time playing it than I have spent with my girlfriends.

But that time wasnt wasted, no.
It was all goddamn worth it.
Every second of every minute of every hour of every day.

It was frustrating, it made me imagine throwing sharp objects at people.
But, more than that, it was something Ive spent months of total playtime on.

Through this game, I came to realize that no time is wasted. Every moment of life is so, so precious. It doesnt matter if you spent it laughing or crying.

It doesnt matter how many times you face the same problem.
You need to face it with strength, hope.
You have the strength you need.
All you have to do is press on.

So I thought Yes, this is it.

And I pressed spacebar.

Playing it was intense.

The level, it didnt exceed my expectations. It was perfect.

A perfect level to end it all.

I spent hours upon hours, just having fun with it.
Grabbing the bottom bounce-block after a fall from the top.
Smashing four rockets at a time against the gray walls.
I spent hours trying to get all the gold. And failed.

This level was beyond me.



All the time while I was playing it, in the back of my head echoed This it the last level Ill ever play.

And, with that thought, I had fun.
I had the time of my life playing it.
I died in more ways than it was possible.
I died more times than I died on the rest of the levels.
I spent hours trying to perfect a single route, then giving up and starting the next one.
All that time, I played with a smile on my face.
I didnt feel frustration for even a moment, because I honestly wished I would never beat this level.

And, after I thought I had squeezed all the fun out of this map I could ever manage to, I decided to actually beat it.

I chose the most insane route I could possibly think of, and executed it.

I was glad that user episodes werent implemented yet.
I didnt want to see you have achieved transcendence after I beat it.
I had already achieved it when I first saw the level.
This, this wasnt about transcendence.
It was all about ending everything with a bang.

I assured myself that I will not back down on this oath.
I will stop playing N not because all of the preparations I had made. I will stop because I want to.

I will not say that this is the best level Ive ever played.
I will say that this is the best level I ever played.
To the immense fun I felt when I played it, no other level can ever compare.

I remembered motherlode. I remembered the first time I played N. I remembered how I couldnt walljump to save my life.
I remembered the time when I reached 5-0, and couldnt beat it.
I remembered when I beat it, and then couldnt beat 5-4.
I remembered 9-1, 19-4, 24-4, 29-3, 37-3
I remembered LLCoolDave, and how he told me the way to beat 39-4. I remembered helping Alex beat it, the same way LLCD helped me.

Long ago, I was thinking that 83-3 will be the end of me.
Then I was thinking 85-3 will be the end of me.
Then it was 86-3.
I remember vividly how I felt when I beat 88-4 for the first time.

As I was playing this level, all the previous levels flashed in front of my eyes. All of the levels I beat before reaching this one.

I thought, that if motherlode was the beginning of it all, this is the end.
motherlode was the alpha, and this is the omega.
And I didnt regret it.
This was truly a level worthy of such a title.



And, after all those hours, when I finally fell down that shaft, I cried.

I cried, I said Yes, this is perfect.

And pressed Q.

Zenith .sol submitted.
N data erased.

Thank you all.

*bows*
